---
title: Tartan Register palette
date: 2023-03-18
---
The Scottish Register of Tartans' standard colour list — every shade the Register records threadcounts against, from its published 'Tartan Register colours' document (Aug 2023 edition); captured in data/sources/str-colours, where shades dropped since the 2016 edition are kept flagged superseded. Codes are numbered in document order where the Register reuses one code for several shades (R1…R11); ref keeps the published code.

Print it: [**the Tartan Register shade card (PDF)**](/colour/tartan-register/tartan-register-shades.pdf) — every shade as a 24 mm swatch with its code, name and the reference values to dye against. Or [**the woven shade card (PDF)**](/colour/tartan-register/tartan-register-shades-woven.pdf) — each dye beside its colour-corrected woven (2/2 twill) reading, the way the sample sheets print a palette.

Like the base-six palette, the Register's shades arrange by intensity — the codes carry it themselves: an L prefix is the light band, a D prefix the dark, W and K the caps.

And plotted over the fine grid in three illuminance layers — light, mid and dark. Each wheel is one lightness layer plotted in OKLab's own dimensions: the angle is hue — red at the top, then clockwise through orange and brown, yellow, green, blue and purple, the wheel's jog order — and the radius is chroma, with rings every 0.05. The faint grey dots are the fine grid at that lightness; the red dashed line is the dyeable limit — at each hue, the strongest chroma any lightness in the layer's band reaches. Hover a dot for its code, name and hex. The small dots are the human palette, the full dots this palette's own shades — so you can read how it lands among the named colours.

## The shades

Each shade in OKLCh — lightness **L**, chroma **C** and hue **h** (degrees). The small number under each is that axis's fine-grid index: the lattice level, chroma ring and snapped hue angle that together make the shade's grid code.
